567	## 7. Blast radius, cutover ordering, and test strategy
568	
569	### 7.1 Blast radius (most → least invasive)
570	
571	| Layer | Change | Invasiveness |
572	|---|---|---|
573	| `packages/core` | Replace `FolderMetadata`/`FileMetadata`/`FilePointer`/`FolderEntry` + vault `encryptedRootFolderKey` with `Node`/`SealedChildRef`/`PublishedNode` + codecs (two sealed bodies, content self-seal, structured write chain) | Highest (keystone) |
574	| `crates/fuse` | Symmetric child-key unwrap; delete `spawn_file_meta_reencrypt`; add `rotateReadFromNode`; unify scope-exit; **grant-root awareness in `delete`/`rename`/`move`**; durable M1 generation + seq high-water; `Node` as Rust enum | High (Rust, two clients) |
575	| `apps/tee-worker` + `packages/core/src/ipns` | **TEE enclave contract rewrite** (lease-renewer: receive marshaled record, verify signature, extend EOL; internal epoch derivation; name↔key binding); republish no longer increments | High |
576	| `packages/sdk` + `sdk-core` | Read-chain navigation; rewrite `shared-write.ts` (structured write-body, role `0x04`); delete `addShareKeys`/`reWrapForRecipients`; rotation driver; `bin/*` re-link; invite claim re-wrap | High |
577	| `apps/web` | Replace `executeLazyRotation` with `rotateReadFromNode`; drop per-mutation fan-out; reconcile `folderTree`; durable M1 generation + seq high-water | Medium-High |
578	| `apps/api` | Delete `share_keys`; slim `shares` (`readDescriptorRef`/`writeDescriptorRef`); rotation bookkeeping; **collapse `ipns_republish_schedule` duplicated columns into `ipns_records`**; **atomic conditional-UPDATE publish CAS**; **tombstone state + publish-gate rejection + resolve tombstone/410 + TEE unenroll**; client-side re-enroll/re-wrap recovery path; `resolveRecord` fail-closed fall-through; **rename `folder_ipns` → `ipns_records`** (entity `IpnsRecord`, repository) and **drop `folder_ipns.public_key`** | Medium-High |
579	| `packages/crypto` | Add `sealAesGcmAad`/`unsealAesGcmAad` + `buildNodeAad` (TS + Rust twin + KAT; frozen byte encoding incl. role `0x04`) | Low (additive) |
580	
581	`folder_ipns` → `ipns_records`: the table holds the IPNS records for files, root, bin, and the vault-key blob too, not just folders — rename it (free under greenfield). Drop `folder_ipns.public_key`: it is the raw 32-byte Ed25519 IPNS pubkey (`ipns.service.ts:72-79` validates length 32 and `deriveIpnsName(pubkey) === ipnsName`), not the user's secp256k1 `publicKey` (the owner is tracked by `userId`); it is null for shared-folder rows and derivable from the k51 name via `publicKeyFromIpnsName`, so drop the nullable column and always recover from the name (removes the null-row footgun behind two Phase-60 regressions).
582	
583	### 7.2 Buildable cutover order
584	
585	1. `packages/crypto` — `sealAesGcmAad`/`unsealAesGcmAad` + `buildNodeAad` (TS) + byte-identical Rust twin + a committed cross-language KAT fixture (frozen byte encoding, role `0x04`) asserted by both. Self-contained, no consumers break.
586	2. `packages/core` — `Node`/`SealedChildRef`/`PublishedNode` + codecs, two sealed bodies, content self-seal, structured write chain, `versionFloor`. Keystone — nothing below typechecks until done.
587	3. `packages/sdk-core` — read-chain navigation + `rotateReadFromNode` driver (in named files, not a fat `index.ts` barrel — coverage excludes barrels). Rebuild dist before consumers.
588	4. `packages/sdk` — `shared-write.ts` rewrite (structured write-body, (c) full rotation); delete `addShareKeys`/`reWrapForRecipients`; `bin/*` re-link; invite claim re-wrap.
589	5. `apps/api` — delete `share_keys`; slim `shares`; rename `folder_ipns` → `ipns_records` + drop `public_key`; collapse the schedule's duplicated columns; atomic publish CAS; tombstone state + publish-gate rejection + resolve fail-closed fall-through. Run `pnpm api:generate`, commit the regenerated client (pre-commit `check-api-client.sh`).
590	6. `apps/tee-worker` — lease-renewer contract (verify marshaled record, extend EOL, no increment); internal epoch derivation; name↔key binding. Round-trip the TEE/republish E2E.
591	7. `apps/web` — `executeLazyRotation` → `rotateReadFromNode`; drop per-mutation fan-out; reconcile `folderTree` against `sequenceNumber` before publishes; durable M1 generation + seq high-water.
592	8. `crates/fuse` — symmetric unwrap; delete reencrypt; unify scope-exit; grant-root awareness; durable client floors; strict-verify each republish; `Node` enum. Budget a Windows CI round-trip for winfsp (`windows/*` can't compile on macOS; `Cargo Check & Test (Windows)` is authoritative; watch the `super::` vs `super::super::` nesting trap, and the `platform/windows/write_ops.rs:1182` reencrypt twin).
593	
594	Strict-verify caveat: recover the Ed25519 pubkey from the k51 name via `publicKeyFromIpnsName`, **never** from the (now-dropped) `public_key` column. Each rotation republish must round-trip the verified chokepoint.
595	
596	### 7.3 Test strategy (must-pass-before-merge first)
597	
598	1. **Rotation crash-safety / resume (the suite that must exist before merge).** Deterministic fault-injection that aborts the walk after each node and asserts: (i) the revoked recipient can't unwrap from root after the root step; (ii) re-run converges; (iii) no incorrect double-bump. Extend `tests/sdk-e2e` (the only real client→API IPNS publish/resolve round-trip) with abort-and-resume cases.
599	2. **CRIT-1 content-key rotation.** Rotate a file node, publish a new version, assert a holder of the **old** `readKey`/`fileKey` cannot decrypt the new version.
600	3. **HIGH-3 multi-rooted re-mint.** Independently-shared single file inside a deleted/moved subtree — assert the inner grantee's `readDescriptorRef` is re-minted (not orphaned), and a revoked recipient is cut.
601	4. **HIGH-4 add-during-rotation.** Concurrent upload during rotation — assert the new child is not dropped (re-merge on 409).
602	5. **M1 generation downgrade.** Relay serves a stale signed record post-rotation — assert the client fails closed on generation regression (durable high-water survives restart).
603	6. **AAD transplant resistance.** Replay a valid `readKeySealed` under a different `childId`/`role`/`generation` — assert `unsealAesGcmAad` rejects.
604	7. **TS↔Rust AAD KAT.** One committed fixture asserted by both `packages/crypto/__tests__` and a Rust `#[test]` — a byte mismatch is silent total decryption failure.
605	8. **CTR content + version.** A CTR content and a CTR `VersionEntry` both decrypt under the v3 content schema.
606	9. **Scope-exit only.** A private delete/move (no covering grant) performs **zero** rotations (pure relink); a shared delete rotates + revokes.
607	10. **Bin restore.** Restore is a pure re-link (no re-encrypt).
608	11. **Invite claim.** Claim re-wraps the share-root `readKey` to the claimer; revoke (rotate) cuts the link and all claimers at once.
609	12. **Republisher stale-CID.** Republisher re-signs mid-rotation → assert the revoked CID is never re-signed and never served; assert republish does **not** increment the sequence.
610	13. **Within-generation rollback.** Relay serves an old lower-seq same-generation signed record → client rejects via the seq high-water.
611	14. **First-contact / cold-device rollback.** Fresh client with no local high-water → the `SealedChildRef` `versionFloor` rejects a below-floor seq.
612	15. **`parseCachedRecord`-null fall-through.** An unparseable canonical DB row → resolve fails closed (or applies the seq floor), never serving an ungated network record.
613	16. **Concurrent forward publishes.** Two devices at the same `dbSeq` → exactly one 409, zero lost updates.
614	17. **Lease-renewal racing a forward publish.** The renewal never regresses `latestCid`/`sequenceNumber`.
615	18. **TEE name↔key binding.** A swapped wrapped key / wrong-name slot → the enclave refuses to emit.
616	19. **TEE epoch self-derivation.** An attacker-supplied `currentEpoch` is ignored; re-wrap only targets an enclave-valid epoch.
617	20. **Tombstoned name.** Writes to a tombstoned name are rejected; resolve returns the tombstone, not stale content.
618	21. **winfsp read-path.** `gh workflow run "Cargo Check & Test (Windows)"` is authoritative.
619	
620	Keep checker subagents to static analysis only (no concurrent vitest — RAM starvation).
621	
622	## 8. Forward-looking capability-layer fit (Tier 3 — not required)
623	
624	Bottom line: the Tier-1 core is cleanly extensible to the speculative agent-capability ideas (TTL, per-file/read-only scope, op-count caps), the extension point is the write/grant plane and never the read chain, and building any of it now would be premature. The deliverable stands on Tier 1+2 alone.
625	
626	What is already delivered by Tier 1 (not future work): **per-file and read-only scope.** Single-file shares fall out of content self-sealing (Section 2.9); read-only-vs-write is the `permission` column plus the omitted write body. Nothing to add later.
627	
628	What is sound to extend later, for free: the grant row is already `O(recipients)` and column-extensible — adding `ttl`/`opCap`/`capabilityId` columns is a non-breaking migration; `generation` already gives per-node revocation granularity.
629	
630	What is unsound and must not be built as described: **read-side TTL/op-caps are cryptographically unenforceable.** Once a reader holds key + CID, IPFS serves the content forever; a "read expires in 1h" claim is security theater unless you re-encrypt + rotate at expiry, which is just scheduled read-revocation (the Section 4 machinery), not a cheap TTL. Time-boxing and op-caps are meaningful **only on the write path**, and only if a mediated mechanism is ever chosen.
631	
632	The two cheap, non-committal hooks already in this design: the write body is sealed and separable (the forward-compat surface), and revocation routes through `rotateReadFromNode` keyed on `generation`. Do **not** add `ttl`/`opCap`/`capabilityId` to `Node` or `SealedChildRef`, and do not name an `authzTokenId` as the hook (that pre-decides mediated writes). If a capability layer is ever built, it attaches to the write/grant plane, is unenforceable on reads, and needs nothing in the node schema. If Tier 3 is never pursued, nothing here is wasted.
633	
634	## 9. Decisions resolved and remaining open questions
635	
636	### 9.1 Resolved by the grilling sessions (ADRs + decisions)
637	
638	- **Write mechanism** → **(c) full Ed25519 rotation**, ratified (ADR 0001). (Full (a) mediated remains the documented runner-up with explicit flip conditions, Section 5.4.)
639	- **Terminology** → `readKey` / `writeKey` adopted; `folderKey` / `fileKey` / `rootFolderKey` retired; `shares` row uses `readDescriptorRef` / `writeDescriptorRef`; `folder_ipns` → `ipns_records`; `public_key` column dropped (CONTEXT.md).
640	- **Move-within-scope cost** → FUSE gains grant-root awareness and computes **exact per-grant scope** (Section 3.5); benign within-scope moves do not over-rotate.
641	- **Migration** → none; greenfield, `node/v3` is the sole codec (Section 1.6).
642	- **Republisher / resolve / TEE** → DB-canonical resolve with M1 + seq-floor authority; republish never increments; TEE is a lease-renewer; atomic publish CAS; tombstone rotated-out names (Section 6).
643	
644	### 9.2 Remaining open questions
645	
646	1. **Co-writer offline handling.** Under (c), a co-writer offline during a write-key rotation cannot write until they re-fetch the re-wrapped key (Section 5.3). Accepted as explicit, or is a grace/notification mechanism wanted?
647	2. **Rotation host.** Eager million-node rotation is owner-online and resumable; desktop (FUSE) is the natural host. Is it acceptable that a pure-web user without the desktop app pays a long, chunked, multi-session rotation for a large revoke?
648	3. **Write-recipient deletions vs owner-held sub-shares.** When a write-recipient (C) deletes, moves out, or overwrites a node inside a shared folder that the OWNER had *independently* sub-shared to a third party (e.g. a single file shared to D), C can unlink it immediately (C holds the folder's write keys and signs the folder publish) but **cannot** cryptographically revoke D's grant — only the owner holds that node's rotation keys and authority over the `shares` rows. So the unlink and the revocation are split across two principals. Options: (a) leave the sub-share dangling until the owner's next sync runs a reconciliation/rotation pass — bounded exposure window in which D retains read access to the now-binned snapshot (already irreducibly readable via IPFS, ADR 0002, so arguably acceptable); (b) block a write-recipient from destroying a node that carries owner-owned sub-shares — but that requires the relay to tell the writer "this node has active grants," leaking share existence to a delegate; (c) have C's mutation enqueue an owner-signed revocation request that the owner (or the owner's desktop/TEE-mediated agent) executes on next online. Decide the authority model and the acceptable exposure window. (Surfaced from the FS-permutations walkthrough — flow `write-recipient-c-delete-file1`.)
```

220	## Accumulated Context
221	
222	### Key Decisions
223	
224	See PROJECT.md Key Decisions table for full list with outcomes.
225	
226	Recent for v1.1:
227	
228	- make_temp_queue in crates/sdk/src/queue.rs uses pid+counter (not tid+counter) to prevent inter-run temp dir collisions (Phase 45 P01 Rule-1 fix)
229	- T-45-07 uses root-shortcut path (folder_ipns_name==root_ipns_name) for deterministic resolve_folder_key test without network; marked for #15 extension
230	- T-45-08 placed in crates/fuse/src/lib.rs (not apps/desktop) to keep characterization tests co-located with merge_folder_children under test
231	- Network-first with self-hosted Someguy + DB fallback adopted as IPNS resolution strategy (revised from DB-first during Phase 19 context -- see 19-SCOPING_RATIONALE.md #1)
232	- rootFolderKey DB copy kept as permanent fallback (never drop column, IPFS copy for recovery independence)
233	- BYO-IPFS affects pinning only, all IPNS publishes still route through CipherBox API
234	- PERF requirements split across Phase 18 (server-side, pre-change) and Phase 22 (client + load testing, post-change)
235	- IPFS/IPNS histogram buckets: 1ms-30s exponential (14 buckets); republish batch: 1s-120s (10 buckets)
236	- Source label (db/network) only for resolve operations; empty string for pin/cat/publish
237	- Alloy scrapes Kubo directly via Docker internal network (ipfs:5001), not proxied through API
238	- Kubo Health dashboard panels use fallback Go runtime metrics alongside libp2p metrics pending post-deploy verification
239	- IPNS-specific histograms: resolve 50ms-30s, publish 100ms-60s with source/outcome labels
240	- Null resolve results (not found) excluded from IPNS histogram observations
241	- Used axios-functions orval client for @cipherbox/api-client (plain functions, no React deps)
242	- sdk-core IPFS ops use direct axios/fetch (not api-client) for upload progress; IPNS ops use api-client generated functions
243	- Bin/share operations take explicit context objects (BinOperationContext, ShareOperationContext) instead of Zustand stores
244	- Share module accepts callback functions for API calls to stay transport-decoupled
245	- Moved @cipherbox/core from dependencies to devDependencies in crypto (test-only cross-package assertions)
246	- Kubo pebbleds datastore (LSM-tree) configured via IPFS_PROFILE=server,pebbleds; requires fresh volume on deploy
247	- [Phase 56 P02] publish_with_cas_retry uses sync Fn(u64) closure seam; folder site keeps its own CAS loop (async merge-on-conflict path cannot delegate to sync helper)
248	- [Phase 56 P02] D-01a: per-file/bin publish Conflict exhaustion returns Err→EIO; journal-on-exhaustion deferred (no JournalOp::FilePublish/BinPublish variant)
249	- [Phase 56 P02] D-11: matched_by_stable_id=false clears children_loaded and children to force fresh subtree load on display-name-only fallback match
250	- SDK concurrent pins require pebbleds datastore (synergistic); concurrent pins alone cause regression at 50 clients
251	- Combined per-task commits into single commit due to pre-commit hook requiring api-client regeneration with entity/dto/controller changes
252	- Desktop root folder detected by inode::ROOT_INO at publish call sites (simpler than modifying build_folder_metadata return type)
253	- Desktop initialize_vault produces v2 blob for new users from day one (not just on migration)
254	- decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs_public transparently handles both v1 JSON and v2 binary blobs
255	- VaultExportDto returns only rootIpnsName and derivationMethod (crypto columns dropped)
256	- Recovery tool IPNS resolution uses gateway /ipns/ HEAD request with redirect following (most reliable without API dependency)
257	- fetchAndDecryptMetadata handles both v1 JSON and v2 binary blobs transparently for folder sync
258	- Zero-crypto vault schema: server stores only ownerPublicKey and rootIpnsName, all crypto material lives exclusively in IPFS v2 blobs
259	- DB crypto columns (encrypted_root_folder_key, encrypted_root_ipns_private_key, migrated_at) fully dropped -- no fallback paths
260	- PinningProvider interface: KuboProvider uses Basic auth, PsaProvider uses Bearer auth, matching each protocol's native auth model
261	- PsaProvider.pin() throws intentionally; pinByCid() is the correct PSA workflow (CID-reference-only protocol)
262	- Connection test uses sequential probe: Kubo /api/v0/id first, then PSA /pins, with 10s timeout per probe
263	- CID registration gated to BYO users only via ForbiddenException (non-BYO users cannot bypass upload relay)
264	- Advisory quota: checkQuota() always true for BYO, getQuota() includes advisory boolean flag for UI display
265	- pinFn injection pattern: optional pinFn parameter on sdkCore.uploadFile() replaces addToIpfs when BYO mode active
266	- External+Kubo bypasses CipherBox entirely; external+PSA uses relay for CID only; dual does both with best-effort secondary
267	- PsaProvider.pinByCid() accessed via cast in client.ts (PSA-specific, not on PinningProvider interface)
268	- Migration uses existing BullMQ pattern with pin-migration queue name; TEE decrypts ECIES-encrypted provider configs in-enclave with epoch key
269	- SSRF protection on TEE migration: validates URL structure (HTTPS-only, no private IPs) and DNS resolution (rebinding check)
270	- BYO config stored as encrypted IPNS entry using rootFolderKey -- no server-side credential storage (zero-knowledge preserved)
271	- Dedicated IPNS key derived via HKDF with context string byo-ipfs-config from vault keypair
272	- BYO benchmark execution (21-07 Task 4) deferred -- requires external IPFS provider infrastructure; test scenarios ready to run when provider available
273	- BYO config loaded at login via IPNS resolve with graceful fallback to cipherbox-only mode
274	- Source unpin is best-effort and non-fatal after verified CID transfer to destination
275	- Cargo workspace with centralized deps at repo root; cipherbox-crypto crate as foundation for all Rust SDK extraction
276	- Module re-export pattern in desktop crypto/mod.rs preserves all existing crate::crypto::* paths without touching call sites
277	- cipherbox-core crate layered on cipherbox-crypto: folder, file, bin, vault_blob, ipns, registry, decrypt, error modules
278	- File module re-exports FileMetadata types from folder.rs (shared AES encryption context with parent folder key)
279	- decrypt module moved from fuse to crypto re-export (domain logic, not FUSE-specific)
280	- Hand-structured API client crate rather than openapi-generator (modest API surface, proven code, no Java/Docker CI dependency)
281	- critical-section std feature required for standalone ecies linking (Tauri provides it in desktop builds)
282	- Shared test vectors in tests/vectors/ JSON files loadable by both Rust and TypeScript for CI parity gates
283	- SyncDaemon uses Arc<dyn Fn(SyncStatus)> generic callback instead of Tauri AppHandle for testability
284	- Desktop api/client.rs re-exports cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient as type alias to unify types across modules
285	- Desktop AppState wraps Arc<KeyState> from SDK; all key material accessed via state.sdk.*
286	- Keychain operations kept as desktop-specific keychain.rs module (not in api-client crate)
287	- Desktop api/ and crypto/ directories fully removed; all imports use workspace crates directly
288	- CI parity gate uses needs.changes.outputs.src (not nonexistent packages) for trigger condition
289	- Desktop-e2e binary paths updated to target/debug/ to match workspace cargo build output
290	- PinataProvider uses dual base URLs: uploads.pinata.cloud (fixed) for upload, api.pinata.cloud (configurable) for management
291	- pinWithMode treats Pinata like Kubo: direct upload bypasses CipherBox relay entirely
292	- Connection test probe order updated: Kubo -> Pinata -> PSA; pinata.cloud URLs skip Kubo probe
293	- BYO Pinata baselines: pin p50=2.0s (+47% vs local Kubo), tail latency p99 13.5% better, 98% CipherBox API load reduction per file
294	- perf.ts PERF_ENABLED evaluated once at module load (zero overhead in production); **CIPHERBOX_PERF** global for opt-in production debugging
295	- Load test thresholds set at 2-3x observed baselines; spike test most generous (15s/15%); vitest expect() for CI failure on breach
296	- Write-share authorization in upsertFolderIpns falls through to create-new-entry when no write share found (preserves backward compat for owner first publish)
297	- TEE enrollFolder uses existing.userId (FolderIpns owner) for write-share publishes, not authenticated userId
298	- Per-file IPNS records created for shared uploads (same as owner uploads) instead of empty fileMetaIpnsName PoC shortcut
299	- File IPNS private key dual-wrapped: owner key in FilePointer, recipient key in share_keys (keyType: file-ipns)
300	- addShareKeys API relaxed to allow write-share recipients to add keys to their own share
301	- TextEditorDialog has separate shared file save path via onSaveSharedFile callback
302	- Shared file download/view falls back to fileKeyEncrypted from metadata when no share_key exists
303	- FilePointer resolution uses FileMetadata directly (no separate ResolvedFileMetadata struct)
304	- FilePointer resolution scoped to parent folder via get_unresolved_file_pointers_for_parent() to avoid wrong-folder-key decryption
305	- FilePointer async resolution: 500ms base * 2^attempt exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s) with 3 retries
306	- Removed custom dstack-sdk.d.ts since @phala/dstack-sdk@0.5.7 ships own TypeScript types
307	- Defensive CVM key derivation handles both key (v0.5+) and asUint8Array (legacy) SDK return types
308	- TEE worker Prometheus metrics use `cipherbox_tee_*` prefix for Grafana dashboard coexistence with API metrics
309	- TEE worker structured JSON logger has zero external dependencies (JSON.stringify to stdout/stderr)
310	- [Phase 48-05] Share itemName encrypted at rest via additive nullable item_name_encrypted bytea on BOTH shares and share_invites (decision A3 includes invite flow); migration is additive-only with NO data UPDATE (server zero-knowledge cannot re-encrypt legacy plaintext); itemNameEncrypted optional hex DTO on create-share/create-invite/claim-invite; claim re-wraps ephemeral→recipient ciphertext onto the Share; web encrypt/decrypt/lazy-backfill deferred to 48-06
311	- [Phase 48-06] Web ECIES-wraps itemName on share/invite create (recipient pubkey for direct, ephemeral pubkey for invite) and sends ciphertext-only (itemName: '' + itemNameEncrypted) -- no plaintext display name at rest for new rows; recipient decrypts itemNameEncrypted into the store's plaintext projection on received-share load so display sites are unchanged; owner sent-list uses plaintext fallback (zero-knowledge: name wrapped for recipient, owner can't decrypt -- T-48-18 accept). API GAP: no update endpoint accepts itemNameEncrypted, so the legacy lazy-backfill (A2) is detect+re-wrap only; persist blocked pending a follow-up API plan (PATCH itemNameEncrypted)

338	### Open Concerns
339	
340	- 6 LOW-priority tech debt items remain from M2 audit: Settings URL param parsing, OCC coverage, addManyFiles atomicity, conflict telemetry, lazy rotation, desktop E2E (see `.planning/milestones/m2/m2-v1.0-production-MILESTONE-AUDIT.md`)
341	- Recovery tool subfolder recovery limited by IPNS DHT propagation (root-level fully operational; per-file IPNS records may not be resolvable if not propagated -- architectural limitation, not a bug)

343	  - Q1 (Phase 68): Co-writer offline during write-key rotation -- accept explicit re-fetch requirement or add grace/notification?
344	  - Q2 (Phase 63): Rotation host for pure-web users -- is a long chunked multi-session web rotation acceptable for large revokes, or is desktop the only host?
345	  - Q3 (Phases 65, 68, 69): Write-recipient-vs-owner sub-share authority -- when C (write recipient) deletes a node the owner independently sub-shared to D, who controls revocation of D?

375	## Decisions
376	
377	- [Phase 59-04]: F.1 next_file_publish_sequence(is_first_publish=true) returns 1; unified with TS SDK first-publish convention
378	- [Phase 59-04]: F.2 replay.rs child-folder first-publish embeds seq=1; record_publish seeds at 1 for coordinator consistency
379	- [Phase 59-04]: F.3 verify.rs skew allowance (resp_seq==1 && embedded_seq==0) removed; strict embedded_seq == resp_seq (T-59-10)
380	- [Phase 59-04]: F.4 ipns_verify_vectors case-8 expected_result changed valid->invalid; classify_vector uses strict equality
381	- [Phase 59-04]: TEE re-sign path confirmed NOT hitting upsertFolderIpns embedded-seq gate (T-59-11 accepted); no API/TS change needed
382	- [Phase 59-03]: D.1 journal_entry if/else body collapsed to single Err; param kept with D-01a TODO
383	- [Phase 59-03]: D.3 current_seq_for_cas replaced by direct if current_seq.is_none() guard (same error text)
384	- [Phase 59-03]: E.1 VerifiedResolve::signature_verified field removed; was never read, only written
385	- [Phase 59-03]: E.4 bytesToHex helper removed alongside the unused public_key/private_key vector fields
386	- [Phase 59-02]: VerifyError::Legacy carries { cid: String, sequence_number: String } from bind_verified -- no second resolve_ipns in any Legacy arm (T-59-04 TOCTOU eliminated)
387	- [Phase 59-02]: Display for VerifyError::Legacy includes cid and seq: 'legacy record: all signature fields absent (cid={cid}, seq={sequence_number})'
388	- [Phase 59-02]: events.rs synthetic VerifiedResolve keeps signature_verified: false until Finding E.1 (plan 03) removes the field
389	- [Phase 45-04]: IpnsResolveOutcome lives in error.rs with #[derive(Debug)] only -- not thiserror, it is an outcome not an error

10	## Problem
11	
12	Surfaced by the Phase-63 security audit as **FLAG-63-U1** (non-blocking).
13	
14	`rotateOne` in `packages/sdk-core/src/rotation/engine.ts` (~L329-330) passes `new Uint8Array(32)` (all-zeros) as the `writeKey` argument to `sealNode`. This is **safe in Phase 63**: `unsealNode` without a write key produces a node with no `writeBody`, and `sealNode` skips the write-body reseal when there is no `writeBody` (confirmed at `packages/core/src/node/seal.ts` ~L118). Phase 63 is read-chain only.
15	
16	Latent risk: if a Phase-65+ code path invokes `rotateOne` on a node that **does** carry a `writeBody` before the real write-chain key is wired, the write body would be resealed under an all-zeros key. The Phase-65 seam comment in `engine.ts` (~L324-328) acknowledges this.
17	
18	## Solution
19	
20	When Phase 65 adds write-chain rotation:
21	
22	- Thread the node's real `writeKey` (or a freshly minted `writeKey'`) into `rotateOne` instead of the all-zeros placeholder, and reseal the write body under it.
23	- Remove the `PLACEHOLDER_WRITE_KEY` and its seam comment once the real key is wired.
24	- Add a test covering rotation of a node that has a `writeBody`.
25

9	
10	## Problem
11	
12	Flagged Major (Security & Privacy) by the Phase-63 PR CodeRabbit review (`registration.ts:42-101`, an outside-diff body comment).
13	
14	`createSubfolder` accepts `teeKeys` (the TEE-encrypted IPNS private key + key epoch used so the TEE worker can republish the subfolder's IPNS record), but the publish path does not set `encryptedIpnsPrivateKey`/`keyEpoch`, and the function returns them unset. A new subfolder created this way would therefore not be picked up by the TEE republisher and its IPNS record would eventually expire.
15	
16	Phase 63 is the read-chain/rotation skeleton and intentionally does not wire TEE republishing (TEE lease-renewer contract is Phase 67; the app is non-runnable mid-milestone). The risk is silent: a caller that supplies `teeKeys` expecting republish wiring gets a subfolder that is not republish-enrolled, with no error.
17	
18	## Solution
19	
20	Either:
21	- **Fail closed now (cheap guard):** if `teeKeys` are supplied to `createSubfolder` but the publish path does not yet wire them, throw a clear "TEE republish wiring not implemented (Phase 67)" error rather than silently publishing without them — so no caller mistakenly believes a subfolder is republish-enrolled. (Low-risk; can be done before Phase 67 if any caller starts passing teeKeys.)
22	- **Wire it (Phase 67):** thread `encryptedIpnsPrivateKey` + `keyEpoch` into the published record and the return value as part of the TEE lease-renewer integration.
23	
24	Verify whether any current caller passes `teeKeys` (grep) — if none do, this is latent param surface and the fail-closed guard is sufficient interim hardening.
25	
26	## References
27	
28	- CodeRabbit PR #579 review, `registration.ts:42-101`
29	- CLAUDE.md TEE Republishing architecture; ROADMAP Phase 67 (TEE lease-renewer contract)
30

Let me present the domain boundary and the genuine gray areas.

---

## Phase 65: SDK Write-Chain, Bin Re-link, and Invite Claim

**Domain:** The write-plane crypto in `packages/sdk-core` + `packages/sdk`. Four deliverables: (1) the structured recursive **write-chain** in the write-body (Ed25519 signing material sealed under a separate `writeKey`, role `0x04`); (2) **write-revocation** = full Ed25519 rotation per ADR 0001 (new keypair + k51 name per node, parent re-point cascade to share root, tombstone old names); (3) **bin restore** as a pure re-link; (4) **invite claim** re-wrap of a single root `readKey`. Plus deleting `addShareKeys`/`reWrapForRecipients`/`encryptedChildKeys`/`originalFolderKeyEncrypted`.

**Locked already (design doc §2.2/§3.10/§3.11/§5 + ADR 0001 — not re-asking):**
- Write mechanism = **(c) full Ed25519 rotation** (ADR 0001, ratified). Read/write keys independent; the read chain is invariant.
- Write-body = structured recursive chain; write link lives in the **parent write-body** (`writeChildren[].writeKeySealed`), never in `SealedChildRef`. Role `0x04`.
- Bin restore = re-seal node `readKey` under destination parent; delete `originalFolderKeyEncrypted` + re-encrypt path.
- Invite claim = unwrap root `readKey` with URL-fragment ephemeral key, re-wrap to claimer; delete `encryptedChildKeys[]` fan-out. Revoke = rotate the `readKey`.

**Carrying forward from Phase 64:**
- **Mock-seam discipline (D-04):** Phase 65 stays in `sdk-core`/`sdk`; live `shares` schema + publish-gate are Phase 66, tested behind injected callbacks.
- **Fail-closed over placeholders (D-01):** the rotation engine already rejects all-zero/malformed IPNS keys — Phase 65 wires the *real* `writeKey` (folds the `rotateone-placeholder-writekey-phase65` todo).
- **sdk-e2e manual-node pattern** + **terminal-owner-only zeroization** + engine stays in `engine.ts` (coverage excludes barrels).

The remaining gray areas are genuinely open:

## Phase 65 — the 4 open decisions, with my recommended call

**1. Q3 — writer-vs-owner sub-share authority** *(ROADMAP mandates deciding this phase; cascades to 68/69)*
When write-recipient **C** deletes/moves a node the **owner** independently sub-shared to **D**, C can unlink immediately (holds the folder write keys) but can't revoke D (owner-only authority).
→ **Recommend (a): leave D dangling until the owner's next sync runs a reconcile+rotation pass.** Zero new infra, consistent with ADR 0002 (the binned snapshot is already irreducibly readable via IPFS), and avoids leaking share-existence to a delegate (which kills option b). Document the bounded exposure window. Option (c)'s owner-signed revocation queue is a real feature — defer it as a noted idea, don't build it now.

**2. Phase 65/66 transport boundary**
→ **Recommend: hold the Phase-64 line.** Phase 65 stays in `sdk-core`/`sdk`; the tombstone enforcement (publish-gate reject, resolve 410) and `writeDescriptorRef` co-writer persistence are **mock-tested behind injected callbacks**; the live DB/publish-gate cutover is Phase 66. WRITE-04's success-criterion text names apps/api surfaces, but the cutover order (§7.2) puts those at step 5 = Phase 66. Phase 65 produces the tombstone *intent* + removes names from the TEE batch via the unenroll callback.

**3. Co-writer offline handling**
→ **Recommend: explicit SDK error only this phase** ("cannot write until re-fetch"). Grace-period/notification UX is open question **Q1, already assigned to Phase 68** — don't pull it forward. WRITE-03 itself says "explicit."

**4. Write-revocation e2e proof scope**
→ **Recommend: extend `tests/sdk-e2e` with a real write-chain rotation round-trip** (new k51 per node, parent re-point cascade to share root, tombstone-intent) as the phase gate — mirrors Phase 64's TEST-01. Unit-only would leave the heaviest operation in the system unproven end-to-end.

**Folded todo:** only `rotateone-placeholder-writekey-phase65` (wire the real `writeKey`, drop the placeholder). The rotation follow-ups (`rotation-coderabbit-followups-deferred`, RR-01 merge, RR-02 resume) are correctly Phase 66/68; `createsubfolder-tee-republish-wiring` is Phase 67.

---

14	## discuss_areas (default, interactive)

16	For each selected area, conduct a focused discussion loop.
17	
18	**Research-before-questions mode:** Check if `workflow.research_before_questions` is enabled in config (from init context or `.planning/config.json`). When enabled, before presenting questions for each area:

27	📊 Best practices research:

29	• Session tokens with httpOnly cookies preferred over localStorage for XSS protection
30	• Consider passkey/WebAuthn support — adoption is accelerating in 2025-2026

34	
35	When disabled (default), skip the research and present questions directly as before.
36

38	check whether to continue. Each answer should reveal the next question.

40	**For each area:**
41

48	   - header: "[Area]" (max 12 chars — abbreviate if needed)
49	   - question: Specific decision for this area
50	   - options: 2-3 concrete choices (AskUserQuestion adds "Other" automatically), with the recommended choice highlighted and brief explanation why

55	     - List (simpler, would be a new pattern)

58	   - Include "You decide" as an option when reasonable — captures Claude discretion
59	   - **Context7 for library choices:** When a gray area involves library selection (e.g., "magic links" → query next-auth docs) or API approach decisions, use `mcp__context7__*` tools to fetch current documentation and inform the options. Don't use Context7 for every question — only when library-specific knowledge improves the options.

61	3. **After the current set of questions, check:**
62	   - header: "[Area]" (max 12 chars)

64	   - options: "More questions" / "Next area"
65

67	
68	   If "More questions" → ask another 4 single questions, then check again

70	   If "Other" (free text) → interpret intent: continuation phrases ("chat more", "keep going", "yes", "more") map to "More questions"; advancement phrases ("done", "move on", "next", "skip") map to "Next area". If ambiguous, ask: "Continue with more questions about [area], or move to the next area?"

This is the decisive grounding. Critical findings that sharpen the scope:

- **Phase-62 codec already supports the write-body** — `seal.ts` handles `writeBody`/`writeSealed`/role-`0x04`/`encodeWriteBody`/`decodeWriteBody`, `sealNode(node, readKey, writeKey)`, `unsealNode(…, writeKey?)`. **Phase 65 *calls* the codec, doesn't build it** (mirrors the "fill seams, don't re-architect" discipline).
- **`shared-write.ts` is fully stubbed** — every fn throws `"not implemented — phase 65 (write-chain)"`. This is the core implementation target.
- **`reWrapForRecipients` is gone from the sdk layer** (Phase 63) — only the **apps/web** copy remains → Phase 68. `addShareKeys` apps/api endpoint → Phase 66; its client.ts `addShareKeysFn` callback type is quarantined to Phase 68. So the roadmap's "delete addShareKeys/reWrapForRecipients/encryptedChildKeys" splits across 65/66/68 **by layer** — exactly per the boundary you locked.

  "content": "# Phase 65: SDK Write-Chain, Bin Re-link, and Invite Claim - Context\n\n**Gathered:** 2026-06-29\n**Status:** Ready for planning\n\n<domain>\n## Phase Boundary\n\nPhase 65 builds the **write plane** in `packages/sdk-core` + `packages/sdk`, on top of the Phase-62 `node/v3` codec and the Phase-63/64 read-chain rotation engine. Four deliverables (WRITE-01..04, ADR 0001):\n\n**In scope (WRITE-01, WRITE-02, WRITE-03, WRITE-04):**\n\n- **WRITE-01 — structured recursive write chain.** Implement the stubbed `packages/sdk/src/share/shared-write.ts` (every export currently throws `\"not implemented — phase 65 (write-chain)\"`). Each node's write-body holds its Ed25519 signing material; each **parent write-body** seals the child `writeKey` (`writeChildren[].writeKeySealed = AES-GCM(child.writeKey, key=parent.writeKey, role=0x04 child-writekey)`). The write link lives in the parent **write-body**, never in `SealedChildRef` (which stays read-only, one sealed field `readKeySealed`). A read-only holder (only `readKey`/`readDescriptorRef`) can never reach signing material — verified by attempting a write-body unseal with only the `readKey`.\n- **WRITE-02 — write-revocation = (c) full Ed25519 rotation** (ADR 0001). New Ed25519 keypair + k51 name **per node**, cascading parent re-points **upward** to the share root, re-pointing surviving co-grants and owner devices. This is a **distinct, heavier** operation than read-rotation (design §5.3: \"not a strict subset of the read-rotation machinery\").\n- **WRITE-03 — surviving co-writer re-key.** Rotated Ed25519 key re-wrapped into each surviving co-writer's `writeDescriptorRef`; an offline co-writer gets an explicit \"cannot write until re-fetch\" error on next attempt.\n- **WRITE-04 — tombstone the rotated-out IPNS name** (intent + client-side removal from the TEE republish batch via the unenroll callback). The **live** publish-gate rejection + resolve-410 enforcement is mock-tested here, cut over for real in Phase 66 (see D-02).\n- **Bin restore = pure re-link** (`BinEntry` re-sealed under destination `readKey`, identical to a move); delete `originalFolderKeyEncrypted` + its re-encrypt-on-restore path from `packages/sdk/src/bin/index.ts` (the `node/v3` `BinEntry` already carries `nodeRef`, not the legacy fields — Phase 62 [62-05]).\n- **Invite claim = re-wrap a single root `readKey`.** On claim, unwrap `readKey` with the URL-fragment ephemeral private key, re-wrap to the claimer's public key → a standard `shares` grant. Delete the `encryptedChildKeys[]` fan-out path from the invite-claim **logic** (sdk-core/sdk). Revoke = rotate the `readKey` (cuts the link and all claimers at once).\n- **Wire the real `writeKey`** into the rotation engine's Phase-64 `nodeKeySource` seam (folds `rotateone-placeholder-writekey-phase65`).\n- **TEST-01-style gate:** a real `tests/sdk-e2e` write-chain rotation round-trip (D-04).\n\n**Out of scope (hard boundary — owned by later phases):**\n\n- **All apps/api surface** — live `shares` schema (`readDescriptorRef`/`writeDescriptorRef` columns), `share_keys`/`addShareKeys` deletion, `encryptedChildKeys` column drop, tombstone state machine + publish-gate enforcement + resolve-410, atomic publish CAS, `folder_ipns` → `ipns_records` rename → **Phase 66**. Phase 65 exercises these behind injected callback seams (D-02 / Phase-64 D-04 discipline).\n- **All apps/web surface** — `reWrapForRecipients` (now only in `apps/web/src/services/share.service.ts`), the `addShareKeysFn` callback type, `executeLazyRotation` → `rotateReadFromNode`, co-writer offline grace/notification UX (open question Q1), durable M1/seq high-water → **Phase 68**.\n- **TEE lease-renewer contract** + `createSubfolder` TEE republish wiring → **Phase 67**.\n- **`crates/fuse`** write plane, Q3 FUSE-side authority mirror → **Phase 69**.\n- **The rotation-soundness follow-ups** (RR-01 merge re-enqueue, RR-02 `verifySubtreeClean` depth, grant-threading) → Phases 66/68 (see Deferred).\n\nThe app stays **intentionally non-runnable mid-milestone** (greenfield, single cutover). Do not pull later-phase apps/* deletions or migrations forward to make it runnable.\n\n</domain>\n\n<decisions>\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n### Q3 — write-recipient-vs-owner sub-share authority (D-01)\n\n- **D-01:** **Model (a) — reconcile on owner sync; the exposure window is a documented residual.** (ROADMAP-mandated decision for this phase; mirrors to Phases 68/69.)\n  - When a write-recipient **C** deletes / moves-out / overwrites a node inside a shared folder that the **owner** independently sub-shared to a third party **D**, C unlinks immediately (C holds the folder write keys and signs the folder publish) but **cannot** cryptographically revoke D — only the owner holds that node's rotation keys and authority over the `shares` rows. The unlink (C) and the revocation (owner) are split across two principals.\n  - **Phase 65 behavior:** C's destroy/move path **unlinks + bins** with **no cross-principal revoke attempt** and **no new schema**. The owner's reconcile+rotation pass re-derives the dangling grants from the existing **`shares WHERE rootNodeId IN (destroyed/binned subtree)` enumeration** (the HIGH-3 `reMintGrantsRootedAt` seam, inverted) — it is wired **live in Phase 66/68**, needs **no new marker** here.\n  - **Exposure window:** D retains read access to the now-binned snapshot until the owner's next online reconcile. **Accepted as a documented residual**, bounded by the owner's next online session. Per ADR 0002 the binned content is **already irreducibly readable** via IPFS, so the marginal exposure is the navigation/future-write window, which closes on the owner's reconcile-rotation.\n  - **Rejected:** (b) block C's destructive op — requires the relay to tell C \"this node has active owner grants,\" **leaking share existence to a delegate**. (c) owner-signed revocation request queue — real feature, but new signed-request plumbing; **deferred** as a noted idea, not Phase-65 scope.\n\n### Phase 65/66 transport boundary — and what \"delete X\" means per layer (D-02)\n\n- **D-02:** **Hold the Phase-64 line (D-04 discipline): Phase 65 is `sdk-core`/`sdk` crypto + behavior only; everything DB/transport is mock-tested behind injected callbacks and cut over live in Phase 66.**\n  - **Tombstone enforcement** — Phase 65 produces the tombstone **intent** (mark the rotated-out name, remove it from the TEE republish batch via the unenroll callback) and **mock-tests** the publish-gate rejection + resolve-410. The **live** apps/api publish-gate reject / resolve-410 / tombstone state machine is **Phase 66**.\n  - **`writeDescriptorRef` co-writer persistence** — re-wrap crypto runs behind a **mocked `shares` query + mocked persist callback** (exactly as Phase-64 D-04 did for HIGH-3 read-grant re-mint). Live `shares` columns are **Phase 66**.\n  - **What the ROADMAP's \"delete `addShareKeys`/`reWrapForRecipients`/`encryptedChildKeys`\" means given the layer split** (a deliberate refinement — the physical deletions live in apps/* which are later phases):\n    - `reWrapForRecipients` — **already deleted from the sdk layer in Phase 63**. The only remaining copy is `apps/web/src/services/share.service.ts:469` → **Phase 68**. Nothing for Phase 65.\n    - `addShareKeys` — the apps/api endpoint/service (`shares.service.ts:207`, `shares.controller.ts:277`) → **Phase 66** (schema cutover); the `client.ts` `addShareKeysFn` callback **type** stays quarantined → **Phase 68** (per Phase 63). Phase 65 rewires the sdk add-item / invite-claim **logic** so it no longer depends on the fan-out.\n    - `encryptedChildKeys` — the apps/api invite **column/DTO/service** drop → **Phase 66**; Phase 65 deletes the **invite-claim fan-out logic** in sdk-core/sdk and replaces it with the single-`readKey` re-wrap.\n  - Net: Phase 65 makes these symbols **dead from the client's perspective** (mock-tested); the physical apps/api + apps/web removals ride Phases 66 and 68. This keeps Phase 65 unblocked by the DB and preserves the single-cutover, non-runnable-greenfield discipline.\n\n### Co-writer offline handling (D-03)\n\n- **D-03:** **Explicit SDK error only this phase.** A co-writer offline during write-key rotation gets a clear `\"cannot write until re-fetch\"` SDK error; on next attempt the surviving co-writer re-fetches the re-wrapped `writeDescriptorRef` and proceeds. WRITE-03 itself says \"explicit.\" **No** grace-period / notification / pending-rekey marker — that is **open question Q1, already assigned to Phase 68** (web UX). Do not pull it forward into the SDK layer. The exact error type/shape is Claude's discretion.\n\n### Write-revocation E2E proof (D-04)\n\n- **D-04:** **Real `tests/sdk-e2e` write-chain rotation round-trip is the phase gate** (mirrors Phase-64 TEST-01). Extend the suite with a live round-trip that exercises: a node carrying a `writeBody`, write-revocation minting a **new k51 name per node**, the **parent re-point cascade to the share root**, surviving co-writer re-wrap, and tombstone-**intent** on the rotated-out names. Reuse the Phase-63/64 **manual-node build pattern** (`sealNode` + `addToIpfs` + `createAndPublishIpnsRecord` with known keypairs) — now with **real write-bodies** since the codec supports them. Honor SDK-E2E prereqs (docker stack + `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api dev`; redis on **6380**; temp axios interceptor for real 400s). Unit-only was rejected — the heaviest operation in the system must be proven end-to-end against a real API.\n\n### Write-chain implementation discipline — call the Phase-62 codec; wire the real writeKey (D-05)\n\n- **D-05:** **The `node/v3` write-body codec is Phase-62-complete — Phase 65 CALLS it, never reimplements it** (the \"fill seams, don't re-architect\" discipline from Phases 62/63/64). `packages/core/src/node/seal.ts` already implements: `sealNode(node, readKey, writeKey)` (seals `writeBody` → `writeSealed` under `writeKey`, role `0x01`, only when `node.writeBody` is set), `unsealNode(published, readKey, writeKey?)` (unseals `writeBody` only when `writeKey` supplied), `encodeWriteBody`/`decodeWriteBody`, and role `0x04 child-writekey` for the write link (ADR 0003, KAT-frozen).\n  - Phase 65 work is **wiring + the write-revocation driver**: implement `shared-write.ts` to use the codec's write-body; build the write-revocation cascade (separate from / composed with `rotateReadFromNode`, planner's discretion per design §5.3); and wire the **real `writeKey`** into the rotation engine's Phase-64 `nodeKeySource` seam (`packages/sdk-core/src/rotation/engine.ts` ~L382/L399/L512-523 + the Phase-65 comments at L92/L157/L213/L227), removing the placeholder. The Phase-64 fail-closed guard (rejects all-zero/malformed/wrong-length IPNS keys) stays.\n\n### Bin re-link + invite claim re-wrap (locked by design — D-06)\n\n- **D-06:** **Locked by design §3.10 / §3.11 — restated for implementation.** Bin restore re-seals the node's own `readKey` under the destination parent `readKey` (pure re-link, no content re-encrypt); delete `originalFolderKeyEncrypted` + the re-encrypt-on-restore path (`packages/sdk/src/bin/index.ts`). Invite claim unwraps the share-root `readKey` with the URL-fragment ephemeral key and re-wraps to the claimer (one standard `shares` grant per claimer of the same `readKey`); delete the `encryptedChildKeys[]` fan-out logic. Read/write keys are **independent** — a write-revoke rotates only the write plane and does **not** touch the read chain (ADR 0001 consequences; design §2.2). \"Revoke write but keep read\" composition (downgrade-to-read-only vs full revoke) is a **caller/product** concern (Phase 68), not the SDK primitive.\n\n### Claude's Discretion\n\n- The write-revocation driver shape — a distinct `rotateWriteFromNode` vs an extension of `rotateReadFromNode` (design §5.3 frames write-revoke as structurally heavier: mints new k51 names, cascades **upward**). Planner decides; keep the read chain invariant.\n- Whether Phase 65 un-stubs `createFileMetadata`/`createSubfolder` to emit real write-bodies, or continues the manual-node-build pattern for the e2e. Prefer the minimum that proves WRITE-01..04.\n- The exact co-writer \"cannot write until re-fetch\" error type/shape (D-03).\n- Internal factoring of `shared-write.ts`, the write-chain walk, and how the mocked `shares`/persist + unenroll callbacks are structured (extend the Phase-64 seams).\n- How the e2e injects/verifies the parent re-point cascade and tombstone-intent.\n\n### Folded Todos\n\n- `2026-06-29-rotateone-placeholder-writekey-phase65.md` (area `sdk-core`, `resolves_phase: 65`) — `rotateOne` passes an all-zeros placeholder `writeKey` to `sealNode`. Folded per D-05: wire the **real** `writeKey` from the write-body into the rotation engine and add a test covering rotation of a node that **has** a `writeBody`. ```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/.claude/projects/-Users-myankelev-Code-random-cipher-box/memory/feedback-dont-skip-askuserquestion-after-interrupt.md",
  "content": "---\nname: feedback-dont-skip-askuserquestion-after-interrupt\ndescription: A rejected/interrupted AskUserQuestion is not a signal to switch to plain text — re-issue it\nmetadata:\n  type: feedback\n---\n\nIn a GSD discuss-phase run, the first `AskUserQuestion` (the present-gray-areas multiSelect) was interrupted, then the user re-invoked the exact same `/gsd-discuss-phase` command with no correction text. I inferred they preferred a menu-free plain-text flow and laid the gray areas out as prose. The user pushed back: \"why are the questions being skipped?\" — they wanted the structured question UI.\n\n**Why:** An interrupt/rejection of a tool call, followed by a clean re-invocation of the same command, usually means the user is just re-triggering the flow — NOT expressing a preference against the interaction pattern the workflow prescribes. The discuss-phase workflow's `present_gray_areas`/`discuss_areas` steps require `AskUserQuestion`.\n\n**How to apply:** When a workflow prescribes `AskUserQuestion` and the call is interrupted/rejected without an explicit reason, re-issue the question (or ask how they want to proceed) — do not silently downgrade to plain text or skip the questions. Only use the text flow when the user explicitly asks for it or `--text`/`workflow.text_mode` is set. Note: this user is terse-decisive and took the recommended option on every Phase 63/64/65 question, so leading each option with a clear recommendation is good — but still deliver it through the question UI. Related: [[feedback-pr-creation-hygiene]].\n"
}
```

## Created: `.planning/phases/65-sdk-write-chain-bin-re-link-and-invite-claim/65-CONTEXT.md`

**Decisions locked**
- **Q3 authority** — (a) reconcile-on-owner-sync; C unlinks+bins, no cross-principal revoke; exposure window is a documented residual, reconcile re-derived from the `shares WHERE rootNodeId ∈ destroyed-subtree` seam (wired live in 66/68), no new schema.
- **65/66 boundary** — hold the Phase-64 line: sdk-core/sdk crypto only; tombstone enforcement + `writeDescriptorRef` persistence mock-tested behind callbacks; live apps/api cutover → Phase 66.
- **Co-writer offline** — explicit SDK error only; grace/notification UX → Phase 68 (Q1).
- **E2E gate** — real `tests/sdk-e2e` write-chain rotation round-trip (TEST-01 style).
- **Wiring discipline** — the Phase-62 codec already provides the full write-body surface; Phase 65 *calls* it + wires the real `writeKey` into the rotation engine (folds `rotateone-placeholder-writekey-phase65`).

**Key scope refinement captured (D-02):** the ROADMAP's "delete `addShareKeys`/`reWrapForRecipients`/`encryptedChildKeys`" splits **by layer** — `reWrapForRecipients` is already gone from sdk (Phase 63, only apps/web remains → 68); `addShareKeys`/`encryptedChildKeys` apps/api deletion → Phase 66. Phase 65 rewires the sdk consumers so they're dead.

**Noted for later:** Q3 option (c) owner-signed revoke queue; co-writer UX (68); apps/api + apps/web deletions (66/68); TEE wiring (67); rotation follow-ups RR-01/RR-02 (66/68).

---

4	agent-roles: researcher, planner, checker
5	produces: PLAN.md
6	consumes: CONTEXT.md

30	<runtime_compatibility>
31	**Subagent spawning — top-level Claude Code:**
32	The Agent tool IS available in a top-level Claude Code session. Always spawn
33	gsd-phase-researcher, gsd-planner, and gsd-plan-checker as separate Agent() calls.
34	Never absorb these roles inline. Role separation is required regardless of `--chain`
35	or `--auto` — those options suppress interactive prompts only; they NEVER authorize
36	collapsing plan roles into the orchestrator context.
37	
38	**Backgrounded Claude Code (via manager/autonomous):**
39	The calling workflow (manager.md / autonomous.md) already runs plan-phase inline via
40	Skill() on Claude Code so that the plan-checker subagent can still spawn. plan-phase
41	itself does not need to detect this case.
42	
43	**#1009 caveat (discuss-phase early-exit):**
44	The "display the command and exit" instruction near `## 4` applies only to the
45	discuss-phase early-exit path. It does NOT authorize inline role performance for any
46	plan-phase agents.
47	
48	**Other runtimes:**
49	Do not pre-judge Agent availability by introspection. Always attempt the actual
50	Agent() call for gsd-phase-researcher, gsd-planner, and gsd-plan-checker. Only
51	a real tool-unavailable error returned by Agent() is a reliable absence signal —
52	never stop based on a self-assessed "I think Agent is unavailable." If the call
53	fails with a tool-unavailable error, log the gap and stop — do NOT collapse
54	researcher/planner/checker roles inline. Independent agent contexts are required
55	for the plan-checker gate to be meaningful.
56	</runtime_compatibility>

59	
60	## 0. Git Branch Invariant
61	
62	**Do not create, rename, or switch git branches during plan-phase.** Branch identity is established at discuss-phase and is owned by the user's git workflow. A phase rename in ROADMAP.md is a plan-level change only — it does not mutate git branch names. If `phase_slug` in the init JSON differs from the current branch name, that is expected and correct; leave the branch unchanged.
63

79	
80	When the tdd capability's `workflow.tdd_mode` is active (resolved via the plan:pre render-hooks), the planner agent is instructed to apply `type: tdd` to eligible tasks using heuristics from `references/tdd.md`. The TDD guidance is injected via the tdd capability's contribution hook at §5.6; no inline config-get is needed.
81	
82	When `CONTEXT_WINDOW >= 500000`, the planner prompt includes the 3 most recent prior phase CONTEXT.md and SUMMARY.md files PLUS any phases explicitly listed in the current phase's `Depends on:` field in ROADMAP.md. Explicit dependencies always load regardless of recency (e.g., Phase 7 declaring `Depends on: Phase 2` always sees Phase 2's context). Bounded recency keeps the planner's context budget focused on recent work.
83	
84	Parse JSON for: `researcher_model`, `planner_model`, `checker_model`, `research_enabled`, `plan_checker_enabled`, `nyquist_validation_enabled`, `commit_docs`, `text_mode`, `phase_found`, `phase_dir`, `phase_number`, `phase_name`, `phase_slug`, `padded_phase`, `has_research`, `has_context`, `has_reviews`, `has_plans`, `plan_count`, `phase_status` (#3569), `planning_exists`, `roadmap_exists`, `phase_req_ids`, `response_language`, `granularity`.
85	
86	**If `response_language` is set:** Include `response_language: {value}` in all spawned subagent prompts so any user-facing output stays in the configured language.
87	
88	**File paths (for <files_to_read> blocks):** `state_path`, `roadmap_path`, `requirements_path`, `context_path`, `research_path`, `verification_path`, `uat_path`, `reviews_path`. These are null if files don't exist.
89	
90	**If `planning_exists` is false:** Error — run `/gsd-new-project` first.
91	
92	## 1.5. Closed-Phase Gate (#3569)
93	
94	The init JSON includes `phase_status` — one of `Pending | Planned | In Progress | Executed | Complete | Needs Review`. `Complete` means the phase has all summaries AND a `VERIFICATION.md` with `status: passed`. Replanning a closed phase silently rewrites plan docs that no longer match the shipped code, so the workflow must hard-stop here unless the operator explicitly overrides.
95	
96	Parse `phase_status` from the init JSON, then:
97	
98	```bash
99	FORCE_REPLAN=false
100	if [[ "$ARGUMENTS" =~ (^|[[:space:]])--force([[:space:]]|$) ]]; then
101	  FORCE_REPLAN=true
102	fi
103	
104	if [ "${phase_status}" = "Complete" ]; then
105	  if [[ "$ARGUMENTS" =~ (^|[[:space:]])--reviews([[:space:]]|$) ]]; then
106	    # --reviews on a closed phase is never legitimate — concerns belong in a
107	    # new phase or issue against the closed phase's commits.
108	    cat <<EOF >&2
109	Phase ${phase_number} (${phase_name}) is already CLOSED (VERIFICATION status: passed).
110	/gsd-plan-phase --reviews cannot replan a closed phase. If the review surfaced
111	real concerns, open a follow-up phase or file an issue against the closed
112	phase's commits. There is no --force override for --reviews on a closed phase.
113	EOF
114	    exit 1
115	  fi
116	  if [ "$FORCE_REPLAN" != "true" ]; then
117	    cat <<EOF >&2
118	Phase ${phase_number} (${phase_name}) is already CLOSED (VERIFICATION status: passed).
119	Replanning a closed phase will overwrite plan docs that no longer match the
120	shipped code. If you intentionally want to replan over closed work, re-run
121	with: /gsd-plan-phase ${phase_number} --force
122	
123	Otherwise, to view what shipped, see: ${verification_path}
124	EOF
125	    exit 1
126	  fi
127	  # FORCE_REPLAN=true: continue, but emit a banner so the operator sees the
128	  # decision in the transcript and in any committed plan docs.
129	  echo "WARNING: Replanning CLOSED phase ${phase_number} under --force. Verify the closeout was wrong before committing new plan docs." >&2
130	fi
131	```
132	
133	The gate fires only on `Complete`. `Executed` and `Needs Review` are not gated — those states mean planning was finished but verification did not pass, and replanning is a legitimate next step.
134	
135	## 2. Parse and Normalize Arguments
136	
137	Extract from $ARGUMENTS: phase number (integer or decimal like `2.1`), flags (`--research`, `--skip-research`, `--research-phase <N>`, `--gaps`, `--skip-verify`, `--skip-ui`, `--prd <filepath>`, `--ingest <path-or-glob>`, `--ingest-format <auto|nygard|madr|narrative>`, `--reviews`, `--text`, `--bounce`, `--skip-bounce`, `--chunked`, `--mvp`, `--tdd`, `--granularity <coarse|standard|fine>`, `--force` (override closed-phase gate, see §1.5)).
138	
139	**`--research-phase <N>` — research-only mode (#3042 + #3044).** When this flag is present, parse `<N>` as the phase number (overrides any positional phase argument), set `RESEARCH_ONLY=true`, and treat the rest of this workflow as a research-dispatch only — the planner spawn (step 8), plan-checker, verification, gaps, bounce, and post-planning-gaps blocks all skip on `RESEARCH_ONLY`. Use this for cross-phase research, doc review before committing to a planning approach, and correction-without-replanning loops. Replaces the deleted `/gsd-research-phase` command.
140	
141	In research-only mode, two modifiers control behavior when `RESEARCH.md` already exists:
142	
143	- **`--research`** — force-refresh re-research without prompting. Re-spawns the researcher unconditionally and overwrites the existing RESEARCH.md. (This is the existing `--research` flag's standard "force re-research" semantics, reused here.)
144	- **`--view`** — view-only: print existing `RESEARCH.md` to stdout, do **not** spawn the researcher. Sets `VIEW_ONLY=true`. Cheapest mode for the correction-without-replanning loop. If `RESEARCH.md` does not exist, error with a hint to drop `--view`.
145	
146	```bash
147	RESEARCH_ONLY=false
148	VIEW_ONLY=false
149	if [[ "$ARGUMENTS" =~ --research-phase[[:space:]]+([0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?) ]]; then
150	  RESEARCH_ONLY=true
151	  PHASE="${BASH_REMATCH[1]}"
152	fi
153	if $RESEARCH_ONLY && [[ "$ARGUMENTS" =~ (^|[[:space:]])--view([[:space:]]|$) ]]; then
154	  VIEW_ONLY=true
155	fi
156	```
157	
158	**`--granularity <coarse|standard|fine>` — CLI override (#703).** When present, this value is the resolved granularity passed to the planner — it wins over any per-phase `granularities.<type>` config, top-level `granularity` config, or project defaults. The init JSON always includes a `granularity` field reflecting the resolved value; read it from there. Invalid values (anything other than `coarse`, `standard`, `fine`) cause an error at the CLI boundary.
159	
160	Set `TEXT_MODE=true` if `--text` is present in $ARGUMENTS OR `text_mode` from init JSON is `true`. When `TEXT_MODE` is active, replace every `AskUserQuestion` call with a plain-text numbered list and ask the user to type their choice number. This is required for Claude Code remote sessions (`/rc` mode) where TUI menus don't work through the Claude App.
161	
162	**MVP_MODE resolution.** Resolve `MVP_MODE` once via the centralized `phase.mvp-mode` query verb. Precedence (first hit wins): CLI flag → ROADMAP.md `**Mode:** mvp` → `workflow.mvp_mode` config → false. The verb is the single source of truth — do not re-implement the chain.
163	
164	```bash
165	MVP_FLAG_ARG=""
166	if [[ "$ARGUMENTS" =~ (^|[[:space:]])--mvp([[:space:]]|$) ]]; then MVP_FLAG_ARG="--cli-flag"; fi
167	if [[ "$ARGUMENTS" =~ (^|[[:space:]])--tdd([[:space:]]|$) ]]; then
168	  gsd_run query config-set workflow.tdd_mode true 2>/dev/null || true
169	fi
170	```
171	
172	Defer the `phase.mvp-mode` query until `PHASE` is finalized (after explicit argument parsing/fallback phase detection + validation). The verb returns `true|false`; full result also exposes `source` (`cli_flag` | `roadmap` | `config` | `none`) for diagnostics. Mode is **all-or-nothing per phase** (PRD decision Q1).
173	
174	**Walking Skeleton gate.** When `MVP_MODE=true` AND `phase_number == "01"` AND there are zero prior phase summaries (new project), the planner runs in **Walking Skeleton mode** (per PRD decision Q2 — new projects only). Detect with:
175	
176	```bash
177	WALKING_SKELETON=false
178	if [ "$MVP_MODE" = "true" ] && [ "$padded_phase" = "01" ]; then
179	  PRIOR_SUMMARIES=$(gsd_run query phases.list --pick summaries_total 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
180	  if [ "$PRIOR_SUMMARIES" = "0" ]; then WALKING_SKELETON=true; fi
181	fi
182	```
183	
184	When `WALKING_SKELETON=true`:
185	- Planner is instructed to produce `SKELETON.md` in the phase directory alongside `PLAN.md`. The template lives at `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/gsd-core/references/skeleton-template.md` — the planner reads it when producing SKELETON.md (lazy; not loaded on non-skeleton runs).
186	- The plan must scaffold project + routing + one real DB read/write + one real UI interaction + dev deployment — the thinnest possible end-to-end working slice.
187	
188	**Interaction with `--prd <filepath>`.** `--mvp` and `--prd` compose. The PRD express path (Step 3.5) creates `CONTEXT.md` from the PRD file and continues to research; the Walking Skeleton gate fires independently from the conditions above. When both are active on Phase 1 of a new project, the planner receives `WALKING_SKELETON=true` and PRD-derived context simultaneously — the PRD informs *what the skeleton should prove*. No precedence is needed; the two signals are orthogonal. See [`references/mvp-concepts.md`](../references/mvp-concepts.md) for the broader interaction map.
189	
190	Extract express-path args from $ARGUMENTS: `PRD_FILE` (`--prd <filepath>`), `INGEST_PATH` (`--ingest <path-or-glob>`), and optional `INGEST_FORMAT` (`--ingest-format <auto|nygard|madr|narrative>`, default `auto`).
191	
192	`--prd` and `--ingest` are mutually exclusive. If both are present, error and exit:
193	`Invalid arguments: cannot combine \`--prd\` with \`--ingest\`.`
194	
195	**If no phase number:** Detect next unplanned phase from roadmap.
196	
197	**If `phase_found` is false:** Validate phase exists in ROADMAP.md. If valid, create the directory using `expected_phase_dir` from init (includes `project_code` prefix when set):
198	```bash
199	mkdir -p "${expected_phase_dir}"
200	```
201	
202	Set `phase_dir="${expected_phase_dir}"` after creation.
203	
204	**Existing artifacts from init:** `has_research`, `has_plans`, `plan_count`.
205	
206	Set `CHUNKED_MODE` from flag or config:
207	```bash
208	CHUNKED_CFG=$(gsd_run query config-get workflow.plan_chunked 2>/dev/null || echo "false")
209	CHUNKED_MODE=false
210	if [[ "$ARGUMENTS" =~ --chunked ]] || [[ "$CHUNKED_CFG" == "true" ]]; then
211	  CHUNKED_MODE=true
212	fi
213	```
214	
215	## 2.5. Validate `--reviews` Prerequisite
216	
217	**Skip if:** No `--reviews` flag.
218	
219	**If `--reviews` AND `--gaps`:** Error — cannot combine `--reviews` with `--gaps`. These are conflicting modes.
220	
221	**If `--reviews` AND `has_reviews` is false (no REVIEWS.md in phase dir):**
222	
223	Error:
224	```
225	No REVIEWS.md found for Phase {N}. Run reviews first:
226	
227	/gsd-review --phase {N}
228	
229	Then re-run /gsd-plan-phase {N} --reviews
230	```
231	Exit workflow.
232	
233	## 3. Validate Phase
234	
235	```bash
236	PHASE_INFO=$(gsd_run query roadmap.get-phase "${PHASE}")
237	```
238	
239	**If `found` is false:** Error with available phases. **If `found` is true:** Extract `phase_number`, `phase_name`, `goal` from JSON.
240	
241	Now that `PHASE` is finalized, resolve MVP mode:
242	```bash
243	MVP_MODE=$(gsd_run query phase.mvp-mode "${PHASE}" $MVP_FLAG_ARG --pick active)
244	```
245	
246	## 3.5. Handle PRD Express Path
247	
248	**Skip if:** No `--prd` flag in arguments.
249	
250	**If `--prd <filepath>` provided:**
251	
252	1. Read the PRD file:
253	```bash
254	PRD_CONTENT=$(cat "$PRD_FILE" 2>/dev/null)
255	if [ -z "$PRD_CONTENT" ]; then
256	  echo "Error: PRD file not found: $PRD_FILE"
257	  exit 1
258	fi
259	```
260	
261	2. Display banner:
262	```
263	━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
264	 GSD ► PRD EXPRESS PATH
265	━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
266	
267	Using PRD: {PRD_FILE}
268	Generating CONTEXT.md from requirements...
269	```
270	
271	3. Parse the PRD content and generate CONTEXT.md. The orchestrator should:
272	   - Extract all requirements, user stories, acceptance criteria, and constraints from the PRD
273	   - Map each to a locked decision (everything in the PRD is treated as a locked decision)
274	   - Identify any areas the PRD doesn't cover and mark as "Claude's Discretion"
275	   - **Extract canonical refs** from ROADMAP.md for this phase, plus any specs/ADRs referenced in the PRD — expand to full file paths (MANDATORY)
276	   - Create CONTEXT.md in the phase directory
277	
278	4. Write CONTEXT.md:
279	```markdown
280	# Phase [X]: [Name] - Context
281	
282	**Gathered:** [date]
283	**Status:** Ready for planning
284	**Source:** PRD Express Path ({PRD_FILE})
285	
286	<domain>
287	## Phase Boundary
288	
289	[Extracted from PRD — what this phase delivers]
290	
291	</domain>
292	
293	<decisions>
